<a id="2026-05-13---webview-open-command-histories"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Open Command Histories

### Root Problem

Completed, Pending Publish, and Diagnostics open actions were backend-owned and safe, but their result visibility was mostly immediate status text plus the global command history. After refresh, operators had to leave the owning page to confirm whether a backend-selected open action succeeded, warned, or failed.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\commandHistory.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\completedView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\pendingPublishView.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\diagnosticsView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added page-local command history rendering for `completed.open`, `pending_publish.open`, and `diagnostics.open`.
- Shows recent result status, local-versus-journal source, target, row key when applicable, opened path when reported, and backend message.
- Reuses the existing command-history stream and does not create new open targets.
- Keeps completed/pending row-key selection and diagnostics target allowlists backend-owned.

### Validation

- Focused WebView local API static test: 1 test passed.
- Tauri shell build checker passed WebView JavaScript syntax checks and Rust `cargo check`.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 990 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. The change only renders existing command history entries and does not alter open-command request payloads, backend target allowlists, shell-open behavior, or Tk fallback controls.

## 2026-05-13 - WebView Network Persisted Worker Runtime State

### Root Problem

The WebView Network page explained that live worker-board data remained Tk-owned, but it gave operators no read-only visibility into persisted coordinator/worker runtime state. That made the page less useful during worker recovery, pending done-report investigation, or coordinator restart review even though safe state files already existed.

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\contract_read.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\routes_read.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\read_payloads_workspace.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`

- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added `NetworkWorkersDto` and a read-only `NetworkFacadeMixin.get_network_workers()` query.
- Added `/api/network/workers` to the local API read contract, route table, payload adapter, and Tauri startup contract validation.
- Loaded persisted `coordinator_inflight.json` and `worker_state.json` through backend-owned code only.
- Rendered active worker rows, idle worker stats, session completed/failed counts, local worker crash/done state, and state-file paths on the WebView Network page.
- Explicitly labelled the panel as persisted state, not a live coordinator lifecycle surface.

### Validation

- Focused facade/local API/static WebView tests: 3 tests passed.
- Focused Tauri route-contract parity test: 1 test passed.
- Tauri shell build checker passed WebView JavaScript syntax checks and Rust `cargo check`.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 990 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. The new endpoint is read-only and does not add coordinator/worker lifecycle controls, but it adds a new local API contract route that Tauri startup validation now requires. The payload is intentionally conservative because persisted heartbeat progress can lag the live Tk dispatcher.

<a id="2026-05-08---webview-completed-open-action-gating"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - WebView Completed Open Action Gating

Most WebView command actions disable their controls while a backend request is in flight, but completed-row open actions did not. Double-clicks could stack duplicate `completed.open` requests and trigger repeated shell open attempts.

- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 775 tests passed.

<a id="2026-05-08---tauri-backend-bootstrap-disconnect-diagnostic"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Tauri Backend Bootstrap Disconnect Diagnostic

### Root Problem

If the Python local API backend exited before emitting its bootstrap payload, the Tauri shell reported the same message used for a slow backend timeout. Immediate backend crashes and slow startup are different operational failures, so using the timeout message made startup diagnosis weaker.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- `read_backend_bootstrap()` now distinguishes `RecvTimeoutError::Timeout` from `RecvTimeoutError::Disconnected`.
- Early backend stdout closure now reports `Backend stdout closed before bootstrap payload was received.`
- Timeout behavior remains unchanged for slow backend startup.

### Validation

- Tauri scaffold tests: 21 tests passed.
- Tauri shell build checker passed WebView JavaScript syntax checks and Rust `cargo check`.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 775 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is startup diagnostics only and does not change backend launch, validation, or cleanup behavior.

<a id="2026-05-08---tauri-backend-shutdown-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Tauri Backend Shutdown Diagnostics

### Root Problem

The Tauri shell requested graceful backend shutdown before waiting and killing the backend process, but discarded any shutdown-request error. If the backend was unreachable, refused shutdown, or returned an invalid response, the shell still continued safely but left no diagnostic trail for why it fell through to the process wait/kill path.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- `BackendProcess.shutdown()` now writes a shell diagnostic when `/api/backend/shutdown` fails.
- Existing shutdown behavior is unchanged: after the diagnostic, the shell still waits briefly and then terminates the child process if needed.
- Tauri scaffold coverage now asserts the shutdown diagnostic remains present.

### Validation

- Tauri scaffold tests: 21 tests passed.
- Tauri shell build checker passed WebView JavaScript syntax checks and Rust `cargo check`.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 775 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is diagnostic-only and does not change backend lifecycle ownership or shutdown sequencing.

<a id="2026-05-08---webview-refresh-render-failure-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - WebView Refresh Render Failure Guard

### Root Problem

The WebView refresh loop used `Promise.allSettled()` for backend reads, but unexpected render-time exceptions could still reject the whole refresh without showing a shell-level failure message. That left the interval running but could leave the visible UI stale with only a console error.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- `refreshAll()` now catches unexpected refresh/render exceptions.
- The activity line now shows `Refresh failed: ...` for those top-level failures.
- The existing refresh-health pill is marked failed with a `refresh/render` issue.
- Normal backend read failures still flow through the existing `Promise.allSettled()` per-request diagnostics.

### Validation

- Focused WebView local API static test: 1 test passed.
- Tauri shell build checker passed WebView JavaScript syntax checks and Rust `cargo check`.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 775 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This only adds a top-level failure display path for unexpected refresh/render exceptions. Successful rendering and per-request refresh behavior are unchanged.

<a id="2026-05-08---network-probe-ui-handoff-logging"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Network Probe UI Handoff Logging

### Root Problem

Network tab background probe threads handed results back to Tk with `root.after(...)` without handling scheduler failure. During shutdown, tab teardown, or Tk disruption, those daemon threads could die without a useful diagnostic, leaving coordinator status or auth-test controls stale.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\views\network_tab.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_view_source_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Coordinator health probe UI handoff failures now log a warning.
- Worker auth probe UI handoff failures now log a warning.
- Probe timeout behavior and polling cadence are unchanged.
- Added source-policy coverage for both handoff diagnostics.

### Validation

- Focused Network tab source-policy tests: 4 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 775 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This adds diagnostics around UI-thread handoff failures only. It does not change coordinator probing, auth probing, networking, or pipeline behavior.

<a id="2026-05-08---coordinator-auth-token-persistence-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Coordinator Auth Token Persistence Diagnostics

### Root Problem

Coordinator startup and hot token rotation could generate or apply a new auth token, fail to persist it to app state, and still log as though the token was safely stored. That creates a network-mode trust problem: workers may connect during the current run, then fail after coordinator restart because the token was never saved.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Startup-generated coordinator tokens now log a clear warning when persistence fails and are described as active for the current run only.
- Hot-rotated coordinator tokens now log a clear warning when persistence fails and are not logged as persisted.
- Existing runtime behavior remains unchanged: the generated/rotated token is still active immediately for the current coordinator process.
- Added focused tests for generated-token and rotated-token persistence failure messages.

### Validation

- Focused coordinator auth-token diagnostics tests: 3 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 774 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This changes only operator diagnostics around token persistence failure. It does not change token validation, hot-swap behavior, auth enforcement, or worker compatibility.

<a id="2026-05-08---coordinator-unknown-done-report-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Coordinator Unknown Done Report Diagnostics

### Root Problem

If a worker reported `done` or `released` for a job that the coordinator no longer had in-flight, the coordinator returned `404 not_found` but did not leave a durable coordinator-side cluster-log event. During stale reclaim, duplicate reports, or crash-recovery retry cases, that made it harder to explain why a worker believed it had a job while the coordinator had already moved on.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Unknown completion reports now log a coordinator warning and cluster event `done_not_found`.
- Unknown release reports now log a coordinator warning and cluster event `release_not_found`.
- Existing HTTP behavior remains unchanged: workers still receive `404` for unknown terminal reports.
- Added focused tests for both unknown completion and unknown release reports.

### Validation

- Focused coordinator `/api/done` diagnostics tests: 2 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 772 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This adds durable diagnostics only. It does not change completion, release, retry, stale-reclaim, or queue-removal behavior.

<a id="2026-05-08---worker-heartbeat-visibility"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Worker Heartbeat Visibility

### Root Problem

During distributed worker processing, heartbeat failures and coordinator reclaim responses happened while an encode was already active. Those failures were mostly debug-only or cluster-log-only, so the local operator status could remain stale even when the coordinator connection had degraded or the coordinator had reclaimed the job.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Worker status callback failures now log at warning level instead of debug level.
- Heartbeat POST failures now update the worker status channel with a bounded error.
- Coordinator `reclaimed` heartbeat responses now update worker status before requesting local abort.
- Added focused tests for heartbeat failure visibility and reclaimed-job visibility.

### Validation

- Focused network persistence tests: 3 tests passed.
- Network worker source-policy tests: 2 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 770 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This changes diagnostics/status visibility only. It does not change claim, heartbeat, reclaim, retry, queue, or media-processing semantics.

<a id="2026-05-08---web-api-get-timeout-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Web API GET Timeout Guard

### Root Problem

The WebView refresh coordinator coalesces overlapping refreshes, but the shared browser API client used unbounded `fetch()` calls. A single hung backend read could leave `refreshInFlight` true indefinitely, causing future manual and interval refreshes to queue behind a request that would never settle.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\apiClient.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a shared `fetchWithTimeout()` helper using `AbortController` when available.
- `apiGet()` now applies a default 30 second timeout to backend read calls.
- `apiPost()` keeps the existing long-running command behavior by defaulting to no timeout unless a caller explicitly passes one.
- Timeout failures now raise a clear `Request timed out after ... ms` error for existing page-level refresh/status handling.

### Validation

- Focused application facade suite: 54 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 768 tests passed.
- Tauri shell build checker passed WebView JavaScript syntax checks and Rust `cargo check`.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. GET refreshes can now fail visibly after 30 seconds instead of waiting forever. Long-running POST commands are intentionally unchanged unless a future caller opts into a timeout.

<a id="2026-05-08---atomic-local-api-command-journal-writes"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Atomic Local API Command Journal Writes

### Root Problem

The Local API command journal used a predictable `.tmp` path and `Path.write_text()` before replacing the live history file. That was acceptable for a small diagnostics cache, but weaker than the rest of the V5 state-write pattern and could leave less durable operator feedback after interruption.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\command_journal.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Command history now writes through a unique same-directory temp file.
- The temp file is flushed and fsynced before atomic `os.replace()`.
- Failed saves remove the temp file best-effort and continue logging warnings.
- Existing journal summaries remain bounded and unchanged.

### Validation

- Focused command-journal facade test: 1 test passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 768 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This changes persistence mechanics only. Command history schema and frontend payloads are unchanged.

<a id="2026-05-08---bounded-worker-http-json-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Bounded Worker HTTP JSON Diagnostics

### Root Problem

Worker/coordinator HTTP helpers capped response bodies, but a successful HTTP response with invalid JSON escaped as a raw decode error without URL context or a bounded body preview.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\http_json.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a bounded invalid-JSON preview for successful HTTP responses.
- Invalid JSON now raises `RuntimeError` with the request URL and a clipped response preview.
- Preserved the existing 1 MB response cap.

### Validation

- Focused network persistence tests: 75 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 768 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Valid JSON responses are unchanged. Invalid coordinator responses now produce more actionable, bounded diagnostics.

<a id="2026-05-08---worker-pending-done-report-recovery"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Worker Pending Done Report Recovery

### Root Problem

Keeping `worker_state.json` after a failed coordinator report was recoverable, but it still only preserved `job_id` and `source_path`. On restart, crash recovery could only report the job as failed, even if the worker had actually completed and published successfully before the coordinator POST failed.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker_state.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- `worker_state.json` now optionally persists an exact `pending_done_report` payload.
- Failed worker done/ops/release/metadata/internal-release reports save the exact `/api/done` payload for retry.
- Crash recovery retries `pending_done_report` before falling back to the older crash-failed report path.
- Successful pending-report retry clears worker state and emits a `pending_done_recovered` cluster event.
- Added worker-state and crash-recovery coverage for pending done report success/failure.

### Validation

- Focused network persistence tests: 74 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 767 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium. This changes coordinator recovery behavior after a network drop during terminal worker reporting. It is safer for successful completions and clean releases because the exact terminal payload is retried, but malformed pending state still falls back to the older crash-recovery path.

<a id="2026-05-08---worker-state-cleanup-after-accepted-reports"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Worker State Cleanup After Accepted Reports

### Root Problem

Worker `mark_done()` and release paths cleared `worker_state.json` before confirming the coordinator accepted the `/api/done` report. If the report failed because the coordinator was offline, the local crash-recovery record was already gone.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Worker done, clean release, and internal release paths now clear crash-recovery state only after the coordinator POST succeeds.
- Failed report paths keep the local recovery state and continue surfacing operator-visible warnings.
- Updated network persistence tests to assert successful reports clear state and failed reports do not.

### Validation

- Focused network persistence tests: 71 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 764 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium. This improves recoverability for coordinator network drops by keeping local state until accepted. The follow-up pending-report recovery chunk now persists exact terminal payloads for retry after restart.

<a id="2026-05-08---worker-unstartable-claim-release"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Worker Unstartable Claim Release

### Root Problem

A worker could successfully claim a coordinator job and then fail while constructing the local synthetic `QueueRecord`. The worker logged the failure but did not release the claim, leaving the coordinator to wait for stale-heartbeat reclamation before the file could be tried again.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_worker_source_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a worker helper that cleanly releases claimed jobs that cannot be converted into local work.
- Claim-record construction failures now emit the existing cluster event and immediately release the claim.
- Release success/failure is surfaced through the worker status callback.
- Added focused tests for successful unstartable-claim release and release failure visibility.

### Validation

- Focused network persistence tests: 71 tests passed.
- Focused network worker source-policy tests: 2 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 764 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. The change only affects a failure path after a claim has already been handed out but before local processing can start. It avoids stale-timeout waits, but a permanently malformed claim can still be reissued by the coordinator until higher-level validation or failure policy handles it.

<a id="2026-05-08---coordinator-claim-drain-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Coordinator Claim Drain Guard

### Root Problem

Coordinator shutdown flipped `accepting_claims` to false and advertised that state through `/api/health`, but `/api/claim` itself still scanned and handed out jobs until the HTTP server finished closing.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- `/api/claim` now returns an empty claim response while the coordinator is draining.
- The handler does not scan the queue or mutate in-flight state when `accepting_claims` is false.
- Added focused coverage for the shutdown/drain race.

### Validation

- Focused network persistence tests: 69 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 762 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. Normal claim behavior is unchanged while the coordinator is accepting work. During shutdown, fresh work is now refused instead of being assigned in the teardown window.

<a id="2026-05-08---coordinator-bind-failure-fails-closed"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Coordinator Bind Failure Fails Closed

### Root Problem

If the coordinator HTTP server could not bind its configured address/port, `_start_http_server()` logged an error but did not raise. The controller could then mark the Coordinator API active even though no worker could connect.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Coordinator HTTP bind failures now raise a `RuntimeError` after logging the bind error.
- This routes the failure through the existing `start_coordinator_api()` error handling path instead of producing a false-active coordinator state.
- Added focused network persistence coverage for bind failure.

### Validation

- Focused network persistence tests: 68 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 761 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. Successful coordinator startup is unchanged. Failed bind attempts now fail visibly instead of leaving a dispatcher object that is not actually serving HTTP.

<a id="2026-05-08---transactional-in-flight-registry-load"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Transactional In-Flight Registry Load

### Root Problem

`InFlightRegistry.load()` restored jobs directly into live registry dictionaries while parsing the persisted JSON. A malformed job row or worker-stats entry could raise partway through recovery, leaving partially restored in-memory state.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\registry.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Registry load now builds replacement job, claimed-path, counter, and worker-stat state before taking the registry lock.
- Malformed job rows, incomplete job rows, and malformed worker-stat rows are skipped with warnings instead of aborting the whole valid recovery set.
- Valid recovered jobs still restore as in-flight, and valid worker session stats still appear on the worker board.
- Added focused persistence coverage for mixed valid/malformed recovery JSON.

### Validation

- Focused network persistence tests: 67 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 760 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. Valid registry files load the same way. Corrupt or partially written registry files now recover valid rows instead of risking partial live mutation or total recovery failure.

<a id="2026-05-08---worker-board-refresh-failure-visibility"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Worker Board Refresh Failure Visibility

### Root Problem

The coordinator Network tab's live worker board swallowed failures while scheduling or reading worker-board state. If the dispatcher snapshot failed, the board could remain stale without a visible operator warning.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\views\network_tab.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_view_source_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Worker-board poll scheduling failures now log a warning instead of being ignored.
- Worker-board refresh failures now log the exception and update the board summary label with a visible warning.
- Added source-policy coverage so the visibility behavior is preserved.

### Validation

- Focused network view source-policy tests: 3 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 759 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. The worker-board data path is unchanged. Only failure visibility changed.

<a id="2026-05-08---worker-donerelease-failure-visibility"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Worker Done/Release Failure Visibility

### Root Problem

When a worker finished or released a job but the coordinator `/api/done` POST failed, the failure was written to the Python log only. The operator-facing worker status line could remain idle/normal even though the coordinator had not received the terminal report.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Failed done reports now update the worker status callback with a bounded operator-visible warning.
- Failed clean release and internal release reports now update the worker status callback with the same warning shape.
- `_notify_status()` now tolerates partially constructed dispatcher instances by treating a missing callback as unset.
- Added focused network persistence tests for failed done, release, and internal release reports.

### Validation

- Focused network persistence tests: 66 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 758 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This does not change coordinator claim, retry, or queue mutation semantics. It only exposes an already-existing failure through the existing worker status channel.

<a id="2026-05-08---launch-failure-process-cleanup"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Launch Failure Process Cleanup

### Root Problem

The process launcher could successfully start a PowerShell pipeline/audit/rerun process and then fail while writing ActiveJobs launch state or verifying readiness. In that window, the UI/API caller would see a launch exception, but the child process could continue running without reliable app ownership.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_process_spawn_runner.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_service_process_spawn_runner.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added cleanup for already-started processes when launch bookkeeping or readiness verification raises.
- Uses the existing `kill_process_tree()` service hook when available so process-tree cleanup and ActiveJobs updates stay centralized.
- Leaves already-exited immediate-failure processes alone so existing readiness failure classification is preserved.
- Added focused tests for ActiveJobs write failure, readiness failure while still running, and readiness failure after immediate process exit.

### Validation

- Focused process-spawn runner tests: 4 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 755 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. This intentionally changes failure behavior only after `Popen` succeeds but launch bookkeeping fails. The safer outcome is to stop the just-launched process rather than leave an untracked background pipeline running. Normal successful launches are unchanged.

<a id="2026-05-08---bounded-local-api-error-envelopes"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Bounded Local API Error Envelopes

### Root Problem

Route and static-file exception responses returned raw `str(exc)` text without a size cap. A malformed backend exception could send an oversized error body into the WebView diagnostics path.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\handler_policy.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\static_files_policy.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_api_handler_policy.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_api_static_files_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a shared bounded error-text helper capped at 2 KB.
- Route exception payloads and static-file error responses now preserve useful leading detail and append an ellipsis when truncated.
- Added focused policy coverage for route and static error truncation.

### Validation

- Focused handler/static policy tests: 7 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 752 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Normal short errors are unchanged. Extremely long exception text is now clipped before being served to the frontend.

<a id="2026-05-08---web-bootstrap-placeholder-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Web Bootstrap Placeholder Guard

### Root Problem

The Local API served `index.html` after a blind string replacement. If the bootstrap placeholder was accidentally removed from the static HTML, the Tauri/WebView shell could open a frontend with no API token or backend configuration.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\static_files.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\static_files_policy.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_api_static_files_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added an explicit missing-bootstrap-placeholder response.
- `render_index()` now verifies that the placeholder exists before serving the HTML.
- Missing placeholder now returns a logged HTTP 500 text response instead of silently serving a broken frontend.
- Added focused static-file coverage for the guard.

### Validation

- Focused static-file policy tests: 4 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 752 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Valid `index.html` rendering is unchanged. Broken static assets now fail loudly during local frontend startup.

<a id="2026-05-08---local-api-health-route-exception-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Local API Health Route Exception Guard

### Root Problem

`/api/health` is public and is the first endpoint the Tauri shell checks, but it sat outside the route exception guard used by authenticated API routes. If health generation failed, the request thread could fail without the normal JSON error envelope.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\handler.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Wrapped health payload generation in the same exception logging and JSON error response behavior used by other routes.
- Preserved `/api/health` as a public route.
- Added Local API server coverage for a failing health provider.

### Validation

- Focused application facade/Local API tests: 54 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 751 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Normal health responses are unchanged. Failures now return a logged HTTP 500 JSON payload instead of an unguarded handler exception.

<a id="2026-05-08---web-rename-selection-key-normalization"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Web Rename Selection Key Normalization

### Root Problem

The WebView rename tool used locale-sensitive lowercasing for source-path keys. Path matching should be deterministic and aligned with backend selected-row matching; locale-sensitive comparisons can produce surprising results for certain Unicode casing rules.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\renameView.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Replaced `toLocaleLowerCase()` with locale-independent `toLowerCase()` for rename source-key generation, applied-row cleanup, and path-text replacement.
- Preserved backend-selected apply behavior: the API still receives `selected_sources` and the backend rebuilds/selects the plan before mutation.
- Added static web-asset coverage so locale-sensitive matching does not return.

### Validation

- Focused application facade/static tests: 53 tests passed.
- Tauri build checker with WebView JavaScript `node --check` and Rust `cargo check`: passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 750 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This changes only browser-side key normalization; backend rename selection and filesystem mutation rules are unchanged.

<a id="2026-05-08---web-api-error-parser-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Web API Error Parser Hardening

### Root Problem

The WebView API client assumed every backend response body was valid JSON. If the local backend returned a plain-text error, truncated response, or non-JSON failure page, the UI would surface a generic JSON parse failure instead of the useful HTTP status or backend message.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\apiClient.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Parse response bodies as text first, then attempt JSON parsing.
- Preserve JSON command/error payloads when present.
- Fall back to the first 500 characters of a non-JSON body for failed responses.
- Include `data.message` and the HTTP status as additional error fallbacks.
- Added static web-asset contract coverage for the parser behavior.

### Validation

- Focused application facade/static tests: 53 tests passed.
- Tauri build checker with WebView JavaScript `node --check` and Rust `cargo check`: passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 750 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Successful JSON responses still return the parsed object. The behavior change only improves diagnostics for malformed or non-JSON backend failures.

<a id="2026-05-08---tauri-backend-response-size-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Tauri Backend Response Size Guard

### Root Problem

The Tauri shell's internal backend HTTP helper read responses into a `String` without a size limit. The backend is local, but a malformed diagnostics/settings/history response could still make the shell allocate unbounded memory during startup, close-readiness checks, or shutdown.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a 16 MiB cap for Tauri shell backend HTTP responses.
- Replaced unbounded `read_to_string` with a bounded reader that rejects oversized responses and non-UTF-8 payloads with explicit shell errors.
- Added scaffold coverage so the guard remains part of the shell lifecycle contract.

### Validation

- Focused Tauri scaffold tests: 21 tests passed.
- Tauri build checker with WebView JavaScript `node --check` and Rust `cargo check`: passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 750 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Normal Local API responses are far below the cap. Extremely large responses now fail explicitly instead of risking shell memory growth.

<a id="2026-05-08---command-history-load-sanitization"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Command History Load Sanitization

### Root Problem

New Local API command results were recorded as bounded summaries, but entries loaded from an existing command-history file were accepted as arbitrary dictionaries. A malformed, stale, or manually edited history file could push huge nested payloads back through `/api/commands` into the WebView diagnostics surface.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\command_journal_policy.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_api_command_journal_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added loaded-entry sanitization that preserves the command-history summary fields only.
- Bounded loaded message text, warning/error lists, log-path maps, command names, job ids, severity, refresh hints, and timestamps.
- Dropped arbitrary nested fields such as `data` from loaded history rows.
- Added focused policy coverage for malformed and oversized loaded history entries.

### Validation

- Focused command-journal policy tests: 9 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 749 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Newly recorded command results already used this bounded summary shape. Existing history files still load, but oversized or unrelated fields are clipped or discarded before being served to the frontend.

<a id="2026-05-08---settings-patch-no-op-detection"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Settings Patch No-Op Detection

### Root Problem

The Local API settings patch builder counted a key as changed whenever it appeared in the request, even when the proposed value matched the current backend config. A WebView settings form that posts current values could therefore produce unnecessary diffs, backups, and dirty-state behavior.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_settings_patch_candidate.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Compare proposed values against the current JSON-safe backend value before marking a key as changed.
- Same-value settings patches now preview as no changes and confirmed saves do not write the PSD1 config or create a backup.
- Added regression coverage for same-value preview and save behavior.

### Validation

- Focused application facade tests: 53 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 748 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Real value changes still flow through the same validation and save path. The behavior change only suppresses no-op writes that were previously misclassified as edits.

<a id="2026-05-08---local-api-shutdown-readiness-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Local API Shutdown Readiness Diagnostics

### Root Problem

The Local API shutdown command correctly failed closed when close-readiness verification threw, but the exception was hidden. Tauri/WebView2 would receive only a generic `state="unknown"` warning, leaving the backend log without the underlying reason.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\command_payloads_process.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added backend exception logging when `/api/backend/shutdown` cannot verify close readiness before responding.
- Preserved the existing fail-closed command payload: shutdown still returns a warning result with `safe_to_close=false`, `active_work=true`, and `state="unknown"`.
- Added regression coverage that exercises the command payload directly, verifies the warning envelope, and confirms the exception is logged.

### Validation

- Focused application facade tests: 52 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 747 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Shutdown command semantics are unchanged. The only runtime change is additional diagnostic logging for an already-handled failure path.

<a id="2026-05-08---coordinator-runtime-config-guardrails"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Coordinator Runtime Config Guardrails

### Root Problem

Coordinator runtime policy still trusted `CoordinatorPort` and `CoordinatorHeartbeatTimeoutMins` after settings load. A malformed port could crash coordinator startup, while negative, `NaN`, or infinite heartbeat values could disable or destabilize stale-job reclamation.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator_policy.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added bounded port coercion for coordinator startup: invalid, zero, negative, or out-of-range ports now fall back to `7830` with a warning.
- Added finite-positive heartbeat validation: invalid, negative, `NaN`, and infinite values now fall back to the default 5-minute heartbeat timeout with a warning.
- Aligned the operator share-block port formatter with the same valid 1..65535 range so it does not hand workers an unusable coordinator URL.
- Preserved existing valid numeric settings and existing coordinator bind-address behavior.
- Added regression coverage for invalid coordinator port and heartbeat values.

### Validation

- Focused network persistence tests: 63 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 746 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Valid settings behave the same. The only behavior change is that invalid runtime network settings now fall back to documented defaults instead of crashing coordinator startup or accidentally disabling stale-job recovery.

<a id="2026-05-08---worker-crash-recovery-state-cleanup-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Worker Crash-Recovery State Cleanup Guard

### Root Problem

Worker crash recovery handled corrupt or completed `worker_state.json` files by directly unlinking the state file. If that unlink failed on Windows due to locking, permissions, or transient share behavior, dispatcher startup could fail while trying to recover from a previous interrupted job.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Routed unreadable-state, missing-job-id, and successful crash-report cleanup through the existing guarded `_clear_worker_state()` helper.
- Preserved the important behavior that state remains on disk when the crash-recovery `/api/done` report fails.
- Added regression coverage for unreadable-state cleanup, missing-job-id cleanup, successful report cleanup, and failed-report preservation.

### Validation

- Focused network persistence tests: 63 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 746 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. The recovery report payload and failure-preservation behavior are unchanged. The only behavior change is that cleanup unlink failures no longer crash worker startup.

<a id="2026-05-08---network-runtime-failure-visibility"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Network Runtime Failure Visibility

### Root Problem

Network mode still had a few failure paths that were technically handled but not visible in the right diagnostic surface. If a worker successfully claimed a job but failed to build the local synthetic `QueueRecord`, the coordinator cluster log did not show why the job did not start locally. Coordinator queue-removal scheduling failures after `/api/done`, and worker heartbeat progress snapshot extraction failures, also lacked useful log context.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_coordinator_source_policy.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_worker_source_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a `claim_record_invalid` cluster event when `_make_queue_record()` fails for a claimed job.
- Logged coordinator queue-removal scheduling failures after terminal `/api/done` reports.
- Logged heartbeat progress snapshot extraction failures at debug level.
- Preserved existing worker wait/retry behavior and coordinator queue semantics; this pass only adds diagnostics.
- Added source-policy coverage for the diagnostic paths.

### Validation

- Focused worker/coordinator source-policy tests: 3 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 744 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Claiming, releasing, retrying, heartbeat, and queue mutation behavior are unchanged. The coordinator and worker logs now preserve more context for already-failing paths.

<a id="2026-05-08---tauri-build-javascript-syntax-gate"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Tauri Build JavaScript Syntax Gate

### Root Problem

The Tauri build checker verified the Rust shell with `cargo check`, but it did not syntax-check the static WebView JavaScript assets. As the web shell becomes more modular, a typo in a JS module could survive Python unit tests and only appear when opening the Tauri preview.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added `Test-JavaScriptSyntax` to the Tauri build checker.
- The checker now locates `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets`, runs `node --check` on every `.js` asset, and fails with the offending file path on syntax errors.
- Kept the existing `npm run check` / Rust compile path unchanged.

### Validation

- Focused Tauri scaffold tests: 20 tests passed.
- Tauri build checker with `-SkipLinkCheck`: passed, including WebView JS syntax and Rust `cargo check`.
- Tauri launch/close smoke: passed; backend process was detected and cleaned up after window close.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 741 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Runtime behavior is unchanged; the build checker now fails earlier when static WebView JavaScript is syntactically invalid.

<a id="2026-05-08---pending-publish-open-scan-failure-contract"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Pending Publish Open Scan Failure Contract

### Root Problem

The pending-publish Open command rescans pending manifests before opening backend-selected paths. If that scan failed, the facade returned "row not found," which pointed the operator toward selection refresh instead of the actual pending-publish scan failure.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_pending_publish.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_pending_publish_policy.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_facade_pending_publish_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a dedicated `pending_publish.open` scan-failure command result with error severity, the scan exception text, and the row key that was requested.
- Routed scan exceptions in `open_pending_publish_location()` to the new error result instead of the missing-row warning result.
- Added policy and facade regression coverage.

### Validation

- Focused pending-publish policy tests: 7 tests passed.
- Focused application facade/static tests: 51 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 740 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Successful opens, missing rows, missing paths, and backend allowlisting are unchanged. Only the error classification for scan failure before open is corrected.

<a id="2026-05-08---local-api-request-and-static-asset-guards"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Local API Request And Static Asset Guards

### Root Problem

The local API JSON body reader rejected oversized and non-numeric `Content-Length` values, but it did not reject negative lengths. Passing `-1` into a stream read can read until EOF or block depending on the stream, which is the wrong behavior for a local command API. Static asset resolution also relied on lexical path checks without verifying the final resolved path remained below the asset root.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\http_helpers.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_api_http_helpers.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Reject negative `Content-Length` values with the existing `invalid content length` 400 response before reading from the request body.
- Resolve static asset candidates against the asset root and reject any path whose final resolved location escapes that root.
- Added direct tests for negative lengths, oversized lengths, zero-length JSON-body defaults, and static asset traversal rejection.

### Validation

- Focused API HTTP helper tests: 4 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 739 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Valid requests and static asset URLs are unchanged; malformed negative-length requests now fail fast, and asset resolution now has an additional resolved-path containment check.

<a id="2026-05-08---network-ui-failure-visibility"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Network UI Failure Visibility

### Root Problem

The Network tab still had user-facing failure messages that did not always leave enough log context. The coordinator-token regeneration flow could swallow app-state persistence failures, and coordinator queue refresh failures told the operator to check logs without logging the triggering exception.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\views\network_tab.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_view_source_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added module logging to the Network tab.
- Logged regenerated-token persistence failures and reflected the failure in the operator status text.
- Logged live coordinator token hot-swap failures and changed the status text to tell the operator to save/restart rather than implying the token was applied.
- Logged coordinator queue refresh request exceptions before showing the existing "check Settings and logs" status message.
- Added source-policy coverage to prevent these paths from returning to silent failure wording.

### Validation

- Focused network view source-policy tests: 2 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 734 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Token generation, persistence call, live hot-swap behavior, and queue refresh behavior are unchanged; only failure visibility and status wording changed.

<a id="2026-05-08---webview-refresh-coalescing"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - WebView Refresh Coalescing

### Root Problem

The WebView shell refreshes on a fixed interval and also exposes manual refresh controls. If a backend read became slow, repeated timer/manual refreshes could overlap, increasing UI work and allowing stale response batches to race newer ones.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a `refreshInFlight` guard and one-slot `refreshQueued` flag around the existing `refreshAll` workflow.
- Moved the existing backend read/render body into `refreshAllNow()` so interval and manual refreshes share the same coalescing behavior.
- Queued at most one follow-up refresh when a refresh request arrives while another refresh is in progress.

### Validation

- Focused application facade/static tests: 49 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 732 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. The backend requests and render order inside a refresh batch are unchanged. The only behavior change is preventing overlapping WebView refresh batches.

<a id="2026-05-08---backend-health-capability-alignment"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Backend Health Capability Alignment

### Root Problem

The backend health payload advertised only part of the local API command surface. The stricter `/api/contract` remains the source of truth, but health is still the shell's quick compatibility signal; leaving out command routes such as rename apply, settings save/reload, diagnostics open, and backend shutdown made that signal stale.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_status_policy.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_facade_status_policy.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added health capabilities for diagnostics open, completed open, pending-publish open, rename apply, settings save patch, settings reload, and backend shutdown.
- Strengthened the status-policy capability-copy test to cover the added command capabilities.

### Validation

- Focused status policy tests: 4 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 732 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This only expands the informational health capability list. Route behavior, auth, command validation, and media-affecting actions remain governed by the local API contract and backend facades.

<a id="2026-05-08---mdns-discovery-diagnostics"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - mDNS Discovery Diagnostics

### Root Problem

mDNS coordinator discovery is optional, but when it is used it runs in background threads and talks to OS/network discovery services. Callback failures and Zeroconf cleanup failures could disappear from the operator's normal diagnostic trail, making network setup failures harder to distinguish from "no coordinator found."

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\mdns.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_mdns.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Logged coordinator-advertiser unregister and Zeroconf close failures at debug level during stop/cleanup.
- Routed async discovery callback invocation through a small guarded helper that logs callback exceptions instead of allowing background-thread failures to vanish.
- Added direct tests for callback failure logging and advertiser cleanup diagnostics without requiring the optional `zeroconf` package.

### Validation

- Focused mDNS diagnostics tests: 2 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 732 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Discovery behavior is unchanged; this only adds diagnostic logging around existing best-effort cleanup and callback boundaries.

<a id="2026-05-08---tauri-startup-contract-validation"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Tauri Startup Contract Validation

### Root Problem

The Tauri/WebView2 shell validated backend health before opening the WebView, but health only proves a small set of capabilities. A stale Python backend could report healthy while missing a route that the static WebView shell depends on, causing controls to fail after the operator sees the app window.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added Rust DTOs for the backend contract response and route rows.
- After backend health validation, the Tauri shell now requests `/api/contract`, validates `desktop_local_api_contract.v1`, and verifies required method/path/auth triples before opening the WebView.
- On contract drift, the shell terminates the backend child and fails startup instead of launching a partially functional UI.
- Added source-level scaffold coverage for the new contract validation path.
- Added a drift test proving the Rust required route triples exactly match the Python local API read/command contract.

### Validation

- Focused Tauri scaffold tests: 19 tests passed.
- Rust compile check: `cargo check` passed from `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri`.
- Tauri launch/close smoke: passed; backend process was detected and cleaned up after window close.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 730 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. This only affects the preview Tauri shell startup gate. It intentionally fails earlier if the backend contract is stale or incompatible; the existing Tk shell and media pipeline behavior are unchanged.

The WebView shell had route-string coverage for local API endpoints, but it did not verify that frontend controls exposed every backend-allowlisted target/action. The diagnostics open route already allowed `workspace`, but the diagnostics page did not surface a Workspace button, so a valid backend diagnostic action was reachable only indirectly through the contract/debug view.

- Added static Web/Tauri coverage proving diagnostics, completed-row, pending-publish-row, pipeline-control, pipeline-mode, and schedule-override controls expose every value allowlisted by the local API command contract.
- Kept the frontend as a command-submit surface only; no new arbitrary path opening or media-mutating backend behavior was added.

### Validation

- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 728 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This exposes an already-allowed diagnostics target and adds static contract coverage. It does not alter backend path allowlisting, queue processing, pending publish, rename, or pipeline launch behavior.

<a id="2026-05-08---runtime-artifact-diagnostics-and-close-guard-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Runtime Artifact Diagnostics and Close Guard Hardening

### Root Problem

ActiveJobs reconciliation could silently skip malformed runtime records, close-readiness could fail open if related-process inspection itself crashed, and pending-publish preview did not flag duplicate manifests pointing to the same parked payload or destination. In long-running workstation use, these cases hide the exact artifacts/operators need to inspect before deciding whether it is safe to close, restart, or drain pending publishes.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_process_active_jobs.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_process_guard.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_pending_publish.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_service_process_active_jobs.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_pending_publish_service.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- ActiveJobs reconciliation now returns and logs a warning when the folder cannot be reconciled or a record is malformed.
- ActiveJobs process-completion updates now log when a corrupt record is repaired with default fields.
- Close-readiness now fails closed when related MediaPipeline process inspection, fresh pipeline progress, or fresh audit progress cannot be verified, reporting the verification error instead of treating the shell as safe to close.
- Close-readiness verification exceptions are now also logged through the service logger for related-process, ActiveJobs, pipeline-progress, and audit-progress checks.
- Pending-publish scan now flags duplicate local payload and duplicate server-destination references across manifest rows.
- Control flag reads now log unreadable pause/stop/rescan flag files instead of silently falling back to filesystem mtime.
- Expanded the web Diagnostics page to expose existing backend-allowlisted Open buttons for config file, ActiveJobs, queue snapshot, completed manifest, last stdout/stderr logs, and failure markers.

### Validation

- Focused application facade/static and ActiveJobs helper tests: 54 tests passed.
- Focused ActiveJobs update/runner tests: 12 tests passed.
- Focused pending-publish/facade tests: 57 tests passed.
- Focused process-control tests: 11 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 735 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. The only behavior change is stricter close-readiness when process/progress inspection fails. That may block closing in ambiguous states, but it is the safer default for unattended media processing.

<a id="2026-05-08---network-silent-exception-logging"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Network Silent Exception Logging

### Root Problem

Several network-mode coordinator/worker exception paths still used silent `except: pass` style handling. The highest-risk case was a worker heartbeat discovering that a job had been reclaimed by the coordinator, then failing to schedule the local abort callback without leaving an operator-facing diagnostic trail.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\coordinator.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Logged worker status-callback failures at debug level instead of swallowing them.
- Added a small worker helper for reclaimed-job abort scheduling and logged scheduling failures with exception context.
- Logged coordinator failures when oversized-request responses cannot be sent, inflight-state saves fail after missing local completion or worker release, and completed-job queue-removal scheduling fails.
- Preserved diagnostic context when worker HTTP helpers receive an HTTP error whose response body cannot be read.
- Added focused tests for the worker status-callback and reclaimed-abort diagnostic paths.

### Validation

- Focused network persistence tests: 61 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 720 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. Runtime behavior is unchanged except that previously hidden network-mode failures now produce logs. No queue, encode, publish, or protocol semantics were changed.

`settingsView.js` still owned two distinct responsibilities: rendering the read-only grouped operational overview and managing the mutable settings patch builders/save workflow. That made the Settings web module larger than necessary and mixed low-risk display logic with settings mutation controls.

### Implementation

- Loaded `settingsOverview.js` after shared DOM/formatter helpers and before `settingsView.js`.
- Kept compatibility exports (`configValue`, `buildSettingsOverviewRows`, `renderSettingsOverview`) so existing shell code and tests still resolve the same global names.
- Left settings patch builders, backend-mediated preview/save/reload actions, and mutable settings state in `settingsView.js`.

### Validation

- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 717 tests passed.

Low. This is a frontend ownership split only. It does not alter backend settings validation, PSD1 persistence, risk summaries, or settings patch command routes.

<a id="2026-05-08---web-pending-publish-row-open-actions"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Web Pending Publish Row Open Actions

### Root Problem

The web/Tauri Pending Publish page could inspect parked outputs and start a pending-publish drain, but it lacked the Tk maintenance view's selected-row open actions. Operators could not open a selected parked payload, manifest, destination folder, or source folder from the web shell.

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\command_payloads_files.py`

- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`

- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added stable `row_key` values to pending-publish preview rows.
- Added backend command route `POST /api/pending-publish/open`.
- The command accepts `row_key` plus an allowlisted target only:

- The backend rescans pending publish state and selects the path from the matching row. The frontend never sends arbitrary filesystem paths.
- Added Open Local, Open Manifest, Open Destination Folder, and Open Source Folder buttons to the web Pending Publish page.

### Validation

- Focused pending-publish facade/API/static tests: passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 717 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. The new command is shell-open only and path selection remains backend-owned. The only behavioral change to read payloads is adding `row_key` to pending-publish rows.

<a id="2026-05-08---bounded-release-and-setup-validation"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Bounded Release and Setup Validation

### Root Problem

The release self-test could hang indefinitely while it launched nested PowerShell validation scripts. The concrete hang was in `Verify-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Environment.ps1`, which called `Setup-MediaPipeline.ps1 -ValidateOnly`; that setup validator then blocked on UNC path availability checks when the configured media shares were slow or unreachable.

The release self-test also still expected old local API route-contract leaf modules that had already been intentionally consolidated into `contract_read.py` and `contract_command.py`.

### Files Changed

- `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`
- `Verify-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Environment.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\config\setup.ps1`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a bounded child-process runner to the release self-test for nested PowerShell checks.
- The release self-test now drains stdout/stderr asynchronously, applies explicit per-check timeouts, and terminates the child process tree on timeout.
- Removed stale release-layout/parser expectations for consolidated local API route-contract modules.
- Added a bounded process runner inside the environment verifier for the setup validator.
- Added bounded setup-validation probes for source/output/scratch path existence and output/scratch writability. Slow or disconnected UNC roots now produce explicit validation failures instead of freezing the setup validator.

### Validation

- PowerShell parser checks passed for:
  - `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`
  - `Verify-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Environment.ps1`
  - `ops\pipeline\config\setup.ps1`
- Focused Tauri scaffold/release-gate tests: passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 715 tests passed.
- Direct setup validation now completes and reports the actual environment issue: `SourceMovies`, `SourceTV`, and `Outsource` UNC paths timed out after 12 seconds.
- Release self-test with tool integration and end-to-end smoke skipped now completes without hanging; it fails because the configured UNC roots are unavailable/slow, not because of stale file expectations or orphaned validation processes.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. The behavior change is limited to validation and release checks. On a very slow but reachable network share, the 12-second validation probe may report a timeout even though Windows might eventually resolve the path. That is intentional for unattended release gates, but the timeout can be tuned later if needed.

<a id="2026-05-08---tauri-shell-compile-verification"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Tauri Shell Compile Verification

### Root Problem

The Python and static contract tests were passing, but the Rust Tauri/WebView2 shell also needs periodic compile verification during the transition. `cargo` was installed under the user cargo bin directory but not available on the current shell `PATH`, so the initial `cargo check` command failed before compilation.

### Files Changed

- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Verified the existing Tauri shell with the explicit cargo path: `%USERPROFILE%\.cargo\bin\cargo.exe`.
- Verified the non-destructive Tauri prerequisite checker with the bundled PowerShell at `ops\pipeline\runtime\PowerShell-7.6.0-win-x64\pwsh.exe`.
- Verified the Tauri build checker, which runs `npm run check` and `cargo check --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml`.
- Verified the automated launch/close smoke, which starts the Tauri dev shell, detects the local API backend process, closes the WebView window, and confirms backend cleanup.

### Validation

- `cargo check` in `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri`: passed.
- `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Prereqs.ps1`: passed.
- `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Build.ps1`: passed.
- `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Launch.ps1`: passed.
- `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`: inconclusive; exceeded the 10-minute command timeout while nested validation PowerShell processes were still running. The self-test process tree started by this run was stopped afterward.

### Regression Risk

None. Verification-only chunk.

<a id="2026-05-08---network-worker-done-payload-extraction"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Network Worker Done Payload Extraction

### Root Problem

`network\worker.py` built `/api/done` payloads in four places: crash recovery, internal release, normal completion, and clean shutdown release. Those payloads control retry behavior, terminal queue state, and clean release semantics, so duplicated construction inside the dispatcher made it easier for future worker paths to drift.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker_done.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added `worker_done.py` with builders for crash-recovery, release, and completion `DoneRequest` payloads.
- Updated worker crash recovery, internal release, `mark_done()`, and clean `release()` to use the builders.
- Preserved existing retry semantics: `__retry_on_failure` is copied unless the queue outcome is terminal, and clean releases still set `released=True` with `success=False`.
- Added direct tests for crash-recovery error text, release payloads, successful completion metadata, terminal failure retry suppression, and non-retry claims.

### Validation

- Python AST syntax check for the new worker-done helper, worker dispatcher, and network tests: passed.
- Focused network tests: 56 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: pending for the next gate.

### Regression Risk

Medium. This is intended to be behavior-neutral, but it touches worker completion and release reporting. The focused tests cover the payload fields most likely to affect stuck or looping distributed jobs.

<a id="2026-05-08---network-prior-failure-policy-extraction"></a>

<a id="2026-05-08---tk-controller-type-import-cycle-cleanup"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Tk Controller Type-Import Cycle Cleanup

### Root Problem

The rename table helper and settings form helper imported their owning controllers for type hints. Because the owners also instantiate those helpers, static import analysis reported cycles around rename and settings controller ownership. The cycle was not a runtime crash today because the imports were guarded by `TYPE_CHECKING`, but it was still unnecessary coupling and made the controller graph look more tangled than it needed to be.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\controllers\rename_table_controller.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\controllers\settings_form_controller.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_controllers.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Replaced the type-only `RenameController` owner import with a local `RenameTableOwner` protocol that exposes only `refresh_preview()`.
- Replaced the type-only `SettingsController` owner import with a local `SettingsFormOwner` protocol that exposes only the callbacks and `app` reference used by the form helper.
- Kept controller construction and all Tk UI behavior unchanged.
- Added a focused controller boundary test so these helper modules do not regain owner imports accidentally.

### Validation

- Python AST syntax check for touched controller/test modules: passed.
- Focused controller boundary tests: 84 tests passed.
- Search confirmed no owner imports remain inside the controller implementation files.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: pending for the next gate.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is a type-boundary cleanup only. Runtime ownership and UI callback behavior remain the same.

<a id="2026-05-08---activejobs-close-readiness-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - ActiveJobs Close-Readiness Guard

### Root Problem

The Tauri/WebView2 transition plan identified process lifecycle and shell close behavior as the next highest-value hardening area. Close readiness already checked related running processes plus fresh pipeline/audit progress, but it did not directly treat non-terminal `ActiveJobs` records as a close blocker. That left a gap where the shell could report safe-to-close if the snapshot looked idle while `ActiveJobs` still contained a live, launching, or unverifiable process record.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_process_active_jobs.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_process_active_job_runner.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_processes.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade_process_guard.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\command_payloads_policy.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\command_payloads_process.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_service_process_active_jobs.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_api_command_payloads_policy.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added `active_job_close_block_messages()` to scan recent `ActiveJobs` records for `launching` or `active` jobs.
- Treats live PIDs, missing PIDs, unverifiable PIDs, unreadable records, and malformed records as close-readiness blockers.
- Does not block on records whose PID is definitely gone, allowing normal reconciliation to mark those orphaned.
- Added a `ProcessLifecycleServiceMixin.active_job_close_block_messages()` compatibility wrapper.
- Updated close-readiness guard behavior so `Shell close` is blocked when `ActiveJobs` still reports active work, even if the current snapshot appears idle.
- Updated the backend shutdown command payload so direct shutdown requests still set the shutdown event but return `severity="warning"` and include close-readiness data when active work is detected.
- Deferred the backend shutdown callback briefly until after the JSON command response is built, reducing the chance that the standalone backend stops before the Tauri shell receives the shutdown response.
- Added a Local API lifecycle test for an idle snapshot with a `launching` ActiveJobs record, covering both close-readiness and authenticated backend-shutdown warning behavior.

### Validation

- Focused ActiveJobs, service-wrapper, API payload, Local API lifecycle, and close-readiness tests: 13 tests passed.
- Python AST syntax checks for touched lifecycle/API test modules: passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 680 tests passed.
- Note: direct `py_compile` on `test_application_facade.py` hit a transient Windows `__pycache__` access-denied replace error after focused tests; AST parsing and full unittest discovery both passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. This intentionally makes shell close-readiness more conservative when `ActiveJobs` cannot be verified. A stale malformed active-job record may now cause the shell to warn instead of reporting safe-to-close, but that is safer than silently closing while tracked process state is ambiguous. Existing forced-close behavior remains available through the shell confirmation path.

- Updated the reliability regression gate to assert the extracted IO helper.

- Full release self-test: passed.

Low-medium. The public service methods are unchanged and this fixes a clear save-path bug, but it touches folder sidecar persistence. Risk is concentrated around preserving schema defaults, folder stamping, JSON shape validation, and atomic write behavior.

<a id="2026-05-08---rename-metadata-rollback-restore-fix"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - Rename Metadata Rollback Restore Fix

### Root Problem

`service_rename.py` attempted to restore edited pipeline/rename sidecar metadata during `apply_rename_path_plan()` failure handling by calling `_atomic_write_text()`, but the helper was not imported in that module. The exception was swallowed by the per-file restore warning path, which meant a partially updated sidecar could survive a failed standalone rename even after file rename rollback completed.

### Implementation

- Imported `_atomic_write_text` into `service_rename.py` for the metadata restore path.
- Added a regression test that rewrites the first moved pipeline sidecar, forces the second metadata update to fail, and verifies both sidecar files are rolled back to their exact original text.

### Validation

- Focused rename apply/service unittest suites: 25 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 584 tests passed.
- Standalone reliability regression checks: passed.
- Full release self-test: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This only affects the error path after standalone rename metadata writes have started and a later metadata update fails. It does not change normal rename planning, file move order, sidecar schema content on success, or pipeline processing.
