<a id="2026-05-08---vlc-long-path-junction-timeout-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-08 - VLC Long-Path Junction Timeout Guard

### Root Problem

`service_file_open.py` used `cmd /c mklink /J` to create a temporary short-path junction for VLC playback of very long local media paths, but that subprocess had no timeout. Because file-open actions are operator-visible and can be triggered from the desktop UI, an unexpected hang in the junction creation command could block the open request indefinitely instead of falling back to the native path.

### Implementation

- Added `MKLINK_JUNCTION_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` to `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_file_open_plan.py`.
- Passed the timeout into the VLC junction `subprocess.run()` call in `service_file_open.py`.
- Preserved the existing failure path: timeout or other junction creation failure logs a warning, cleans up the temporary root, and returns the native media path without forcing VLC short-path mode.
- Added focused unit coverage proving the junction creation subprocess is bounded.

### Validation

- Focused file-open/file-open-plan unittest suites: 5 tests passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 572 tests passed.
- Standalone reliability regression checks: passed.
- Full release self-test: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. The only behavior change is that long-path VLC junction creation now gives up after a bounded wait and uses the existing native-path fallback. It does not change VLC executable discovery, normal media open behavior, explorer folder/file open behavior, non-Windows open behavior, or pipeline processing.

<a id="2026-05-07---tauri-shell-backend-health-capability-gate"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Tauri Shell Backend Health Capability Gate

### Root Problem

The Tauri shell trusted the backend bootstrap JSON and opened the WebView immediately after receiving `desktop_local_api_bootstrap.v1`. That proved the backend process started, but it did not prove the local API was serving the expected health contract or exposing the core capabilities the shell relies on.

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`

### Implementation

- Added a `BackendHealth` contract struct in the Tauri shell.
- Added `validate_backend_health()` after bootstrap and before window creation.
- Validates `desktop_backend_health.v1`, `status == ok`, and required capabilities for snapshot, telemetry, diagnostics, close readiness, command history, settings, and process launch commands.
- Terminates the Python backend child if the health gate fails before the window opens.
- Added scaffold tests proving the health endpoint, schema check, status check, and capability checks remain present.

- Tauri Rust build checker (`npm run check` / `cargo check`): passed.

- Release self-test: passed.
- Tauri launch/close smoke: passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. Startup is intentionally stricter; a backend missing required capabilities will now fail visibly instead of opening a partially functional shell. Existing CustomTkinter launch behavior is unchanged.

<a id="2026-05-07---ass-subtitle-encoding-fallback-stabilization"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - ASS Subtitle Encoding Fallback Stabilization

### Root Problem

The bundled tool-integration check intermittently failed while converting a tiny ASS subtitle stream. The converter stopped at the first fallback encoding that `pysubs2` could parse; broad encodings such as `cp1252` can parse structurally damaged or mismatched bytes and still produce no usable dialogue text, causing a false "all events were filtered" failure.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\pipeline\ass_to_srt_cli.py`
- `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added encoding-candidate diagnostics for ASS loads.
- Replaced first-success fallback selection with best-candidate selection based on usable dialogue text count, dialogue count, text length, replacement characters, and existing encoding order as the tie-breaker.
- Kept the existing encoding order, style filtering, karaoke filtering, and SRT rendering behavior otherwise unchanged.
- Added the ASS helper to release self-test layout and Python syntax checks.

### Validation

- Python syntax check for `src\mediapipeline\pipeline\ass_to_srt_cli.py`: passed.
- Bundled tool-integration check repeated 5 times: passed.
- Full desktop unittest discovery suite: 198 tests passed.
- Release self-test: passed.

### Regression Risk

Medium-low. This touches subtitle conversion fallback behavior, but only after the preferred UTF-8 paths fail. The change is designed to avoid choosing a structurally parseable but text-empty fallback.

### Root Problem

The Tauri/WebView2 shell could be launched through developer commands and smoke tests, but there was no explicit project-root launcher for trying the preview. That made the new shell harder to open without making it clear that the current CustomTkinter app remains the production UI.

### Files Changed

- Added a root `Start-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-TauriPreview.bat` that uses bundled PowerShell when available.
- Added a PowerShell launcher that resolves Node, npm, Cargo, and Visual Studio build tools, supports `-InstallNodePackages`, supports `-CheckOnly`, and starts `npm run dev`.
- The launcher clearly identifies the shell as a V5 preview and states it does not replace the current desktop app.
- Added scaffold tests to keep this preview launcher explicit, non-destructive, and documented.

### Validation

- Preview launcher `-CheckOnly`: passed.

- `cargo check` for the Tauri shell: passed.
- JavaScript syntax check with user-installed Node.js: passed.
- Full desktop test suite: 194 tests passed.
- V5 release builder dry-run: passed; no files copied.

### Regression Risk

Low. This adds an opt-in launcher only. It does not alter the default desktop launcher, local API behavior, release build semantics, or media pipeline execution.

<a id="2026-05-07---close-readiness-contract-test-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Close-Readiness Contract Test Hardening

### Root Problem

The native Tauri close guard depends on the backend close-readiness contract, but the backend logic did not yet have focused coverage for key states: idle, active pipeline, active audit, unknown snapshot state, and fresh progress overriding a stale-looking snapshot.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Unknown snapshot state is now treated as unsafe to close so the shell asks for confirmation instead of assuming it is safe.
- Added tests proving idle/completed state is safe, processing state blocks close, audit state blocks close, missing snapshot blocks close, and fresh progress blocks close even if the snapshot status appears completed.

### Validation

- Focused API/static tests: 34 tests passed.
- JavaScript syntax check with user-installed Node.js: passed.
- `cargo check` for the Tauri shell: passed.
- Full desktop test suite: 194 tests passed.
- V5 release builder dry-run: passed; no files copied.
- Tauri launch/close smoke: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is a conservative backend-readiness correction: unknown runtime state now prompts before closing. It does not alter pipeline execution, config persistence, source/scratch/output behavior, or process launch semantics.

<a id="2026-05-07---tauri-backend-http-helper-consolidation"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Tauri Backend HTTP Helper Consolidation

### Root Problem

The Tauri shell had separate raw TCP/HTTP request code for graceful backend shutdown and close-readiness checks. That duplicated URL parsing, timeout handling, bearer-token headers, body handling, and HTTP status validation inside the shell.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Routed graceful backend shutdown through the same bounded `request_backend_json` helper used by close readiness.
- Preserved the existing `POST /api/backend/shutdown` payload and the same child-process cleanup fallback.
- Updated scaffold tests so they assert the shared helper contract instead of the old hard-coded shutdown request string.

### Validation

- Rust formatting: passed.
- `cargo check` for the Tauri shell: passed.
- Focused Tauri scaffold tests: 10 tests passed.
- Tauri launch/close smoke: passed; backend cleanup was verified.
- Full desktop test suite: 191 tests passed.
- V5 release builder dry-run: passed; no files copied.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. This changes shell shutdown internals only. The external shutdown route, token use, timeout behavior, and child termination fallback are intended to remain unchanged.

<a id="2026-05-07---tauri-backend-bootstrap-stdout-tolerance"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Tauri Backend Bootstrap Stdout Tolerance

### Root Problem

The Tauri shell expected the Python local API bootstrap JSON to be the first stdout line. That is normally true, but it made startup brittle if Python, a bundled dependency, or a future startup hook wrote a harmless warning before the bootstrap payload.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Replaced one-line bootstrap reading with a bounded bootstrap scanner.
- The shell now skips and logs non-JSON stdout before bootstrap until it sees a valid `desktop_local_api_bootstrap.v1` payload or the startup timeout expires.
- If JSON parses as a bootstrap payload but has the wrong schema, startup still fails deterministically.
- After bootstrap, stdout/stderr pipes continue draining so the backend cannot block on a full pipe.

### Validation

- Focused API/static tests: 32 tests passed.
- Rust formatting: passed.
- `cargo check` for the Tauri shell: passed.
- Tauri launch/close smoke: passed.
- Full desktop test suite: 191 tests passed.
- V5 release builder dry-run: passed; no files copied.
- Tauri launch/close smoke was rerun after full validation: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This only changes shell startup tolerance around backend stdout ordering. It does not alter backend routes, media processing, config, or process launch semantics.

<a id="2026-05-07---native-tauri-close-guard"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Native Tauri Close Guard

### Root Problem

The web prototype could warn through `beforeunload`, and the backend exposed close readiness, but the Tauri shell itself still treated window close as an unconditional backend shutdown path. A native shell needs to consult backend-owned active-work state before closing so the operator cannot bypass the web warning by closing the desktop window.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\Cargo.toml`
- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added a native close guard in the Tauri `WindowEvent::CloseRequested` handler.
- The shell now calls `GET /api/backend/close-readiness` with the per-run bearer token before shutdown.
- If the backend reports `safe_to_close=true`, close continues normally and the existing graceful backend shutdown path runs.
- If the backend reports active work, the shell shows a Windows-native confirmation dialog before allowing close.
- If close readiness cannot be verified, the shell also prompts before closing instead of silently shutting down.
- The existing `WindowEvent::Destroyed`, app-level exit cleanup, and `Drop` fallback remain intact.

### Validation

- Focused API/static tests: 32 tests passed.
- JavaScript syntax check with user-installed Node.js: passed.
- Rust formatting: passed.
- `cargo check` for the Tauri shell: passed.
- Tauri launch/close smoke: passed; idle close continued normally and backend cleanup was verified.
- Full desktop test suite: 191 tests passed.
- V5 release builder dry-run: passed; no files copied.
- Tauri launch/close smoke was rerun after full validation: passed.
- Restarted the V5 local API on port 8765 and verified `/api/health` returned `ok` with exactly one `local_api_main` process.
- Verified live `/api/backend/close-readiness` returns `desktop_close_readiness.v1`, `safe_to_close=true`, and `state=idle` while no work is active.

### Regression Risk

Medium. This changes native close behavior for the future shell only. It does not affect the current Tk app, pipeline execution, PowerShell behavior, or media files. The main risk is a blocking confirmation dialog if the backend reports active work; that is intentional.

<a id="2026-05-07---web-prototype-partial-refresh-resilience"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Web Prototype Partial Refresh Resilience

### Root Problem

The web prototype refreshed all read endpoints through one `Promise.all` batch. A transient failure in one lower-priority endpoint, such as diagnostics, settings, or report preview, could prevent otherwise-good status, telemetry, queue, or pending-publish panels from updating.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\styles.css`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Replaced the web prototype's all-or-nothing refresh batch with `Promise.allSettled`.
- Snapshot remains the required read for the top-level state; other panels update independently when their endpoint succeeds.
- Added a top-bar refresh-health pill that reports `Refresh: ok`, warning, or failed status and keeps endpoint failure details in the tooltip.
- Existing panel data stays visible if one optional endpoint fails during a refresh.

### Validation

- Focused API/static tests: 32 tests passed.
- JavaScript syntax check with user-installed Node.js: passed.
- Full desktop test suite: 190 tests passed.
- `cargo check` for the Tauri shell: passed.
- V5 release builder dry-run: passed; no files copied.
- Tauri launch/close smoke: passed; backend startup and shutdown verified.
- Restarted the V5 local API on port 8765 and verified `/api/health` returned `ok` with exactly one `local_api_main` process.
- Verified the live backend-served web shell includes the `refresh-health` and `close-readiness` top-bar elements.

### Regression Risk

Low. This is a frontend-only refresh behavior change over existing read endpoints. The main behavior change is positive: partial backend read failures no longer blank every panel.

<a id="2026-05-07---backend-close-readiness-contract-and-web-close-warning"></a>
## 2026-05-07 - Backend Close-Readiness Contract And Web Close Warning

### Root Problem

The Tauri shell could gracefully shut down the local Python backend on window close, but there was no backend-owned contract for deciding whether closing the shell would hide active pipeline, audit, or rerun work. The web prototype also had no operator-facing close warning tied to actual backend state.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\dto.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\facade.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\application\__init__.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\contract.py`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\api\server.py`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\index.html`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`
- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\styles.css`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_application_facade.py`
- `docs\archive\historical-reviews\TAURI_WEBVIEW2_TRANSITION_GROUNDWORK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added `desktop_close_readiness.v1`, a read-only DTO that reports `safe_to_close`, current backend state, active-work status, reason, and warnings.
- Added token-protected `GET /api/backend/close-readiness` with `effect=none` to the local API contract.
- The facade uses existing active-process/progress/audit checks plus snapshot pipeline state so close readiness follows backend-owned runtime evidence instead of frontend guesses.
- The web prototype now renders a top-bar close-readiness pill and installs a `beforeunload` warning when backend state indicates active work.
- This does not change backend shutdown behavior, process controls, pipeline execution, or media-file mutation semantics.

### Validation

- Focused API/static tests: 32 tests passed.
- JavaScript syntax check with user-installed Node.js: passed.
- `cargo check` for the Tauri shell: passed.
- Full desktop test suite: 190 tests passed.
- V5 release builder dry-run: passed; no files copied.
- Tauri launch/close smoke: passed; backend startup and shutdown verified.
- Restarted the V5 local API on port 8765 and verified `/api/health` returned `ok` with exactly one `local_api_main` process.
- Verified live `/api/backend/close-readiness` returns `desktop_close_readiness.v1`, `safe_to_close=true`, and `state=idle` while no work is active.

### Regression Risk

Low-medium. The new route is read-only and the web warning is advisory, but it adds one endpoint to the normal refresh batch. If close readiness ever fails, the existing refresh error path still reports a backend read failure instead of mutating state.
